<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## assistant

**I’d turn this into a conditional-reasoning trainer, with translation as its first stage.** The current drill mostly asks students to recognize a diagram. It does much less to test whether they can construct one, connect several rules, or explain why an inference fails.

I inspected the live preview and reviewed the implementation. No code or production content has been changed.

**There are correctness problems to fix before adding more questions.**

| Finding | What should change |
|---|---|
| Both the original conditional and its contrapositive are accepted among five choices. That is deliberate, but selecting one demonstrates limited understanding. | Separate “translate this statement,” “write its contrapositive,” and “select every equivalent statement” into distinct tasks. |
| Answers are visually blurred before submission, but the correct clauses remain exposed to screen readers. | Keep answer content out of the accessible interface until it is revealed. |
| The “if and only if” item uses a one-direction answer key. Its generated grading rejects valid implications while accepting an incomplete translation. | Represent and assess both directions of biconditionals. |
| The final linking item incorrectly treats the absence of a sufficient condition as ruling out the result. | Rewrite the item and verify every inference. |
| Another explanation incorrectly says the converse and contrapositive coincide for a “not both” statement. | Correct the explanation and audit related content. |

The content errors are in the [question bank](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/content/conditional-rephrase.json:516); the answer-generation problem is in [diagramChoices.ts](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/worlds/switchyard/diagramChoices.ts:96).

**On linking: there is almost none of the kind you want.** The authored bank has 62 items, including three explicitly labeled compound-condition items, one biconditional, and one “two conditionals” item. There are **zero dedicated multi-premise chain-inference tasks**. Some sentences are linguistically difficult, but the response remains essentially the same two-sided diagram.

Here is the progression I would build inside the drill:

| Skill | What the student actually does |
|---|---|
| Extract the rule | Identify the complete conditions, including negations and qualifiers. |
| Build the diagram | Place clauses, choose direction, and add negation without seeing answer choices first. |
| Contrapose | Reverse and negate independently, including AND/OR transformations. |
| Apply the rule | Decide what must follow, what could happen, and what cannot happen. |
| Disprove an inference | Construct a situation where the rule holds but the proposed conclusion fails. |
| Link rules | Connect two, then three or more premises, sometimes after contraposing one. |
| Handle branches | Work through AND requirements, OR alternatives, shared conditions, and disconnected chains. |
| Find the missing bridge | Supply the additional rule that would make a conclusion follow. |
| Repair an argument | Identify the first invalid step and correct it. |
| Read a longer passage | Extract relevant rules from a paragraph, ignore distractions, and justify the conclusion. |

**The most valuable creative additions would be these:**

1. **Make students produce something before recognizing it.** Keep paper as an option, but add a lightweight diagram builder. The present “I’ve diagrammed it” button cannot tell whether they diagrammed anything. A builder can reveal whether the problem was direction, negation, missing words, or a lost conjunct.

2. **Use counterexamples as feedback.** After a reversal, do more than say “necessary is not sufficient.” Ask: “Could the orchids bloom even though the fans never ran?” Have the student find the case that satisfies the original rule and defeats their reversal. That teaches why the move fails.

3. **Introduce “nothing follows” answers.** Students should encounter a necessary condition being satisfied, a sufficient condition being absent, and two rules that cannot be linked. Otherwise, the interface teaches them to force an inference every time.

4. **Use minimal pairs.** Present nearly identical statements such as “Only members may enter” and “The only people required to enter are members.” Ask what changed and why the arrow changes. This tests meaning rather than superficial word spotting.

5. **Make longer questions earn their length.** Add difficulty through relationships, paraphrases, and irrelevant information, rather than just longer sentences. Ask students to identify the bridge between “approved application” in one sentence and “authorization” in another.

For example, a proposed linking exercise could say:

> Every fellowship recipient receives research funding. Anyone receiving research funding must submit an approved proposal. A proposal cannot be approved unless its ethics review is complete. Maya received a fellowship.

Then test several different abilities against those same premises:

- Build the chain from fellowship to completed ethics review.
- Explain why Maya must have completed the review.
- Explain why completing the review does not guarantee a fellowship.
- Identify what follows if someone’s review is incomplete.
- Identify which conclusions remain unsupported.

That gives one passage several meaningful uses. Later, use a fresh passage to test transfer without the scaffolding.

6. **Require a repair after feedback.** Instead of immediately advancing after “Show answer,” ask the student to correct the arrow or identify the missing condition. Then give a fresh, structurally similar item. Record the original error separately from the successful repair.

7. **Replace the fixed 62-question march with focused sessions.** Offer short sets for translation, negation, linking, and mixed practice. Increase structural difficulty after demonstrated success, and revisit recurring errors using new wording.

**Saving needs a deliberate redesign.** I verified that reloading this preview returns to the introduction. Code review also found that “Practice again” runs deliberately skip saving. Ordinary assigned first runs already have per-answer saving and resume support, although I did not test those student flows live. See [ActivityRunner.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/ActivityRunner.tsx:488).

Every student practice session should create a distinct saved run containing:

- The exact questions and their versions.
- First answers, subsequent corrections, hints, and answer reveals.
- Completion position, so interrupted work resumes.
- Error types and time spent.
- A review page showing what the student actually did.

A retry should add history, not replace it. “Resume this session,” “Start a new session,” and “Review previous work” should be separate actions. Teacher previews should stay separate from student mastery records.

My recommended build order is **correct the content and scoring, save every student run, then add diagram construction and genuine linking exercises**. After that, use the saved error patterns to drive targeted practice. That would make the drill substantially more sophisticated while keeping its teaching purpose clear.

## assistant

**Keep the current multiple-choice drill, and add two modes: “Supply the missing conditional” and “Must Be True.”** Preserve paper-first diagramming and make modest improvements to the question screen.

I checked your corpus and created a [20-question draft with five choices and explanations](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/brainstorms/2026-09-16-conditional-linking-question-bank.md):

- **10 missing-link questions:** direct connections, contraposition, negative conditions, AND requirements, and OR branches.
- **10 Must Be True questions:** derive a conditional, apply a stated fact, eliminate an OR alternative, and combine several rules.
- Each mode moves from **variables to words**, ending with short sets of conditional premises.

Every final symbolic formulation passed an exhaustive logic check for **exactly one correct choice**.

The missing-link questions capture what you described. For example:

> **Premises:** D → A; B → L  
> **Conclusion:** ¬L → ¬D  
> **Which conditional makes the conclusion follow?**

The answer is **A → B**. It completes D → A → B → L. Contraposing that chain gives the requested conclusion.

A harder version introduces an AND requirement:

> **Premises:** A → B; (B ∧ C) → D  
> **Conclusion:** A → D

Here, **A → C** supplies the second condition needed to reach D. The explanation shows why B alone is insufficient.

The Must Be True questions include the structures in your screenshots:

> **Premises:** (A ∧ B) → C; C → D; A is true.  
> **What follows?** B → D.

And:

> **Premises:** A → (B ∨ C); A is true; C is false.  
> **What follows?** B is true.

**All four groups remain covered.** I preserved the app’s current map and included unless/no forms. I also covered the rules, universals, guarantees, and requirements wording in your screenshots. Those are different organizing schemes, so I kept their labels distinct.

For the design, I recommend only a few changes:

- Stack the premises clearly and separate the target conclusion.
- Keep five choices, revealed after the paper attempt.
- After submission, show the completed chain and highlight the missing link.
- Give one explanation for the selected error instead of several competing answer panels.
- Save every student run, including repeat practice and unfinished sessions.

The draft is saved and linked from your existing design notes. **The app has not been changed yet.**

Yes. I misunderstood your answer format. You mean:

**Premises:**  
A → B  
C → D  

**Conclusion:** A → D  

**What’s the missing link?**

- D. B → A

## assistant

Here is the implementation plan, in order.

1. **Preserve the existing drill.**  
   Keep its questions, activity identity, assignment links, and saved student work. Add the new practice alongside it.

2. **Create one Conditional Reasoning entry in Activity Center.**  
   Inside it, provide General Conditionals, Linking, Missing Link, and Must Be True. Keep the existing visual style and make the choices easy to navigate.

3. **Standardize the notation.**  
   Use diagonal slashes for negation, ordinary arrows, and written **and/or**. Use spacing and branches instead of logical brackets. Apply this consistently to questions, choices, and explanations.

4. **Correct the current drill’s problems.**  
   Fix the identified content and biconditional-grading errors. Prevent answers from being exposed before submission. Make single-answer questions have exactly one correct choice.

5. **Build the missing-link interaction.**  
   Show premises and a target conclusion. Ask **“What’s the missing link?”** Offer five individual conditionals. After submission, show the correct bridge and explain the completed chain.

6. **Add the question content.**  
   Include all 12 photographed exercises and retain the existing drafted questions. Organize practice from simpler variable relationships to negation, contraposition, compound conditions, and wording across all four groups. Add Must Be True questions using rules and facts. Linking will use your clarified missing-conditional interaction, not completed-chain choices.

7. **Save every student practice session.**  
   Preserve answers, retries, revealed explanations, and unfinished progress. Starting another session must not overwrite previous work. Provide separate **Continue**, **Practice again**, and **Review history** actions.

8. **Test the complete experience.**  
   Verify the logic of every answer set, notation, feedback, navigation, refresh/resume, and repeat-session history. Check desktop and mobile layouts, and confirm the original drill still works.

9. **Publish and verify the live result.**  
   Run the required checks, commit only this work, and publish through the available deployment path. If a backend change requires a credit-spending Lovable action, I’ll identify that specific action before requesting the required confirmation.

**The first working milestone will be the preserved General Conditionals drill plus the new hub and a complete Missing Link flow.** Then I’ll finish the remaining practice, persistence, and verification.

- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).

Use spacing, stacked conditions, and branches to make compound scope clear. Do not flatten a more complex expression into an ambiguous line merely to remove punctuation. Internal grading may retain a structured logical representation without displaying it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cover the four groups without changing their labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe 2026-09-15 copy correction requires four groups and a separate “Required means right” takeaway. The live LSAT U page inspected in this conversation labels its groups as follows; preserve that interface pending any explicit taxonomy change:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Group 1: “Left Sufficient triggers.”\\r\\n- Group 2: “Right Necessary triggers.”\\r\\n- Group 3: “If not,” covering unless, without, until and except.\\r\\n- Group 4: “No A\'s are B,” explained as “All A\'s are not B.”\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s attached LSAT Lab screenshots use a different organizing scheme: rules, universals, guarantees, and requirements. These are not asserted to be the same four labels as the LSAT U page. Cover all four screenshot categories as wording tags while retaining the app\'s existing map. Older corpus taxonomies also differ; this draft does not silently adjudicate them. Symbolic items assess logic and do not count as evidence of indicator-language mastery. The mixed word items cover all four app groups, but this starter bank does not exhaust every indicator or scenario.\\r\\n\\r\\nNever infer arrow direction merely from the location of a word. For example, “A ensures B” means A → B; the result B follows “ensures.” Unless does not by itself mean exactly one, and no does not always mean negate the same visible noun phrase regardless of sentence structure.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Supply the missing conditional\\r\\n\\r\\nCommon instruction: **Diagram the premises and conclusion on paper. Which additional conditional, if assumed, makes the conclusion follow?** Reveal choices after the paper step. Reveal keys and explanations only after commitment.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML1: Connect the open ends\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. C → D.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → B |\\r\\n| B | D → C |\\r\\n| C | B → C |\\r\\n| D | B → A |\\r\\n| E | D → A |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: C.** Starting with A gets you to B. The second premise starts at C and gets you to D. Add B → C and the chain becomes A → B → C → D.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A joins C to B in the wrong direction. B reverses the second premise. D takes you back to A. E reverses the target rather than establishing it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML2: Turn a rule before finding the gap\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. C̸ → B̸.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | D → C |\\r\\n| B | C → A |\\r\\n| C | C̸ → D |\\r\\n| D | C → D |\\r\\n| E | D → A |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: D.** Contrapose C̸ → B̸ to obtain B → C. Now A leads to B and then C. C → D finishes the chain.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A runs from the destination back toward C. B loops back to A. C requires C̸, but the premises give C when A occurs. E begins at D, which is what you still need to reach.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML3: Match the sign as well as the letter\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B̸. C → D.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | B → C |\\r\\n| B | C → B̸ |\\r\\n| C | C̸ → B̸ |\\r\\n| D | D → C |\\r\\n| E | B̸ → C |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: E.** A gives B̸, not B. The bridge must let you move from B̸ to C. Then C gives D.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A requires B. B and C merely make B̸ a result; neither lets you start from B̸ and reach C. D reverses the second premise.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML4: Work backward from a negative conclusion\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: D → A. B → L.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: L̸ → D̸.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | A → B |\\r\\n| B | B → A |\\r\\n| C | A̸ → B |\\r\\n| D | L → D |\\r\\n| E | A → B̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: A.** Adding A → B creates D → A → B → L. Contrapose the chain: L̸ → B̸ → A̸ → D̸. That is the conclusion.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: B points across the gap backward. C starts from A̸, although D supplies A. D reverses D → L. E sends A to B̸, which does not establish L or exclude D when L is absent.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML5: Supply the second required part\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. B and C → D.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → A |\\r\\n| B | A → C |\\r\\n| C | D → C |\\r\\n| D | D → B and C |\\r\\n| E | A̸ → C̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: B.** A already gives B. The second rule needs B and C together. Adding A → C supplies C whenever A occurs, so the full trigger is satisfied and D follows.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A does not give C from A. C and D begin with the result you need to prove. E says what happens without A; it says nothing that supplies C when A occurs.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML6: Cover both alternatives\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B or C. B → D.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | D → C |\\r\\n| B | C → A |\\r\\n| C | C → D |\\r\\n| D | A → B̸ |\\r\\n| E | D → A |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: C.** A guarantees at least one of B and C. The B branch already reaches D. C → D makes the other branch reach D too. Either way, A guarantees D.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A and E begin with D. B sends C back to A without ensuring D. D rules out B when A occurs, forcing C, but still gives no path from C to D.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML7: Put the bridge before the existing chain\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: If a visitor\'s badge is scanned, that visitor is admitted. A visitor is admitted only if that visitor\'s registration is current.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: Every invited guest has current registration.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | Every visitor whose badge is scanned is an invited guest. |\\r\\n| B | Every visitor with current registration is an invited guest. |\\r\\n| C | No invited guest has their badge scanned. |\\r\\n| D | Every invited guest has their badge scanned. |\\r\\n| E | Every admitted visitor is an invited guest. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: D.** Let G = invited guest, B = badge scanned, E = admitted, R = registration current. The premises give B → E → R. Add G → B to obtain G → B → E → R.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A, B and E make guest status a result, not a starting point that guarantees registration. C blocks the badge condition without ruling out guest status or establishing registration.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1 and 2; rules, universals, requirements.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML8: Link through an incompatibility\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: No expired pass is valid. Entering the facility requires a valid pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: Anyone holding only a canceled pass cannot enter the facility.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor this exercise, evaluate the person\'s sole pass. Let C = canceled, X = expired, V = valid, E = permitted to enter using that pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | Every canceled pass is expired. |\\r\\n| B | Every expired pass is canceled. |\\r\\n| C | Every pass that is not canceled is expired. |\\r\\n| D | Every canceled pass is valid. |\\r\\n| E | Every valid pass permits entry. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: A.** No expired pass is valid means X → V̸. Entry requires validity: E → V, so V̸ → E̸. Add C → X and canceled leads to expired, invalid, and therefore no entry using that pass.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: B reverses the needed connection. C addresses passes that are not canceled. D establishes validity rather than excluding entry. E gives another route into the facility, not a reason canceled passes cannot be used.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1, 2 and 4; universals and requirements. The sole-pass condition prevents an unstated second pass from changing the result.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML9: Read “cannot unless” before contraposing\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A shipment cannot leave unless it has been inspected. Inspection guarantees that the shipment is sealed.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: No flagged shipment can leave.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | Every unsealed shipment is flagged. |\\r\\n| B | Every flagged shipment is unsealed. |\\r\\n| C | Every flagged shipment is sealed. |\\r\\n| D | Every uninspected shipment is flagged. |\\r\\n| E | Every sealed shipment can leave. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: B.** Let L = can leave, I = inspected, S = sealed, F = flagged. The premises give L → I → S. Therefore S̸ → I̸ → L̸. Adding F → S̸ establishes F → L̸.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A reverses the bridge. C supplies a necessary condition for departure without ruling departure out. D makes flagged status a result, not a trigger for being uninspected. E supplies permission to leave rather than preventing it.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1, 3 and 4; guarantees and requirements. All statuses refer to the same decision time.\\r\\n\\r\\n### ML10: Combine alternatives, “no,” and “unless”\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: Every finalist submits a sample or completes an interview. No applicant who completes an interview lacks approval. Applicants receive approval unless a review is pending.\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: Every finalist receives approval.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | Every applicant without a pending review submits a sample. |\\r\\n| B | Every applicant with a pending review submits a sample. |\\r\\n| C | Every applicant who submits a sample has a pending review. |\\r\\n| D | Every approved applicant submits a sample. |\\r\\n| E | No applicant who submits a sample has a pending review. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: E.** Let F = finalist, S = sample, J = interview, A = approval, P = pending review. The rules are F → S or J, J → A, and P̸ → A. The interview branch already reaches approval. E supplies S → P̸, so the sample branch also reaches approval. Both alternatives now support F → A.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A points from no pending review to sample, not from sample to no pending review. B and C connect samples with a pending review, which does not establish approval. D reverses a possible connection from samples to approval.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1, 3 and 4; universals, OR branches and negation. “Unless” does not say that a pending review necessarily prevents approval.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Must Be True\\r\\n\\r\\nCommon instruction: **Diagram the premises on paper. Which statement must be true if all the premises are true?** A conditional answer states a rule; it does not assert that its trigger actually occurs.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT1: Chain three conditions\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. B → C.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → A |\\r\\n| B | A̸ → C̸ |\\r\\n| C | A → C |\\r\\n| D | B → A |\\r\\n| E | C → B |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: C.** A gives B, and B gives C. So A → C. A, D and E reverse valid arrows. B incorrectly treats the absence of A as ruling out C.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT2: Find a connection hidden by contraposition\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. C̸ → B̸.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → A |\\r\\n| B | B → A |\\r\\n| C | A → C̸ |\\r\\n| D | A → C |\\r\\n| E | A̸ → C̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: D.** The second premise contraposes to B → C. Join it to A → B. A and B reverse what is established. C negates the result. E infers from the absence of the starting condition.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT3: Use a negative fact\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B. B → C. C is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | A is true. |\\r\\n| B | B is true. |\\r\\n| C | C is true. |\\r\\n| D | At least one of A and B is true. |\\r\\n| E | A is false. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: E.** C̸ gives B̸, and B̸ gives A̸. Here a fact activates the contrapositive chain. Every other option conflicts with the resulting facts.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT4: One part of an AND trigger is already established\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A and B → C. C → D. A is true.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | B → D |\\r\\n| B | A → D |\\r\\n| C | D → B |\\r\\n| D | B̸ → D̸ |\\r\\n| E | D → C |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: A.** A is already established. If B also occurs, both parts of the trigger hold, producing C and then D. B incorrectly treats A alone as enough. C and E reverse arrows. D rules out D merely because this particular trigger is incomplete.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT5: Eliminate one OR alternative\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B or C. A is true. C is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | B is false. |\\r\\n| B | B is true. |\\r\\n| C | C is true. |\\r\\n| D | Both B and C are true. |\\r\\n| E | A is false. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: B.** A guarantees at least one of B and C. C is ruled out, so B must hold. A rejects the remaining alternative. C and D conflict with C̸. E contradicts the stated fact A.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT6: Different branches, same result\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: A → B or C. B → D. C → D.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | D → A |\\r\\n| B | A → B |\\r\\n| C | A → D |\\r\\n| D | A → C |\\r\\n| E | A̸ → D̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: C.** Either available branch reaches D. You do not need to know which branch occurs. A reverses the resulting rule. B and D select one alternative without support. E rules out D when A is absent, even though D could occur another way.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT7: Combine “every” and “only”\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: Every editor reviews submissions. Only certified staff review submissions.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | Every certified staff member is an editor. |\\r\\n| B | No person who is not an editor is certified. |\\r\\n| C | Everyone who reviews submissions is an editor. |\\r\\n| D | Every editor is certified. |\\r\\n| E | Every certified staff member reviews submissions. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: D.** Editor → reviewer. “Only certified staff” makes certification necessary for reviewing: reviewer → certified. Therefore editor → certified. A, C and E reverse established connections. B infers not certified from not editor.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1 and 2; universals and requirements.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT8: Chain through a “no” rule\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: No damaged package is accepted. Every insured package is accepted.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | No insured package is damaged. |\\r\\n| B | Every undamaged package is insured. |\\r\\n| C | Every uninsured package is damaged. |\\r\\n| D | Every accepted package is insured. |\\r\\n| E | Every damaged package is insured. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: A.** Damaged → not accepted. Contrapose: accepted → not damaged. Insured → accepted → not damaged. B and D reverse valid relationships. C treats not insured as enough to establish damage. E connects damage to insurance without support.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 1 and 4; universals and contraposition. Universal rules do not establish that any packages actually exist.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT9: Chain “unless” with “only if”\\r\\n\\r\\nPremises: The machine operates unless the safety switch is off. The machine operates only if cooling is active.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | If the safety switch is off, the machine does not operate. |\\r\\n| B | If cooling is not active, the safety switch is off. |\\r\\n| C | If cooling is active, the machine operates. |\\r\\n| D | If the machine does not operate, the safety switch is not off. |\\r\\n| E | If cooling is not active, the safety switch is not off. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: B.** Let S = switch off, O = operates, C = cooling active. The premises give S̸ → O → C. Contrapose the chain: C̸ → O̸ → S. A adds an unwarranted converse to the unless rule. C reverses the cooling requirement. D and E negate the switch condition incorrectly.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: Groups 2 and 3; requirements. Treat these as stipulated rules about the same operating state, not a claim about real machinery.\\r\\n\\r\\n### MBT10: A short mixed-premise paragraph\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery research grant requires approval. Approval is withheld unless the review is complete. No incomplete application receives a completed review. A completed review guarantees either a standard award or an exception. This application receives a research grant but does not receive a standard award.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich statement must be true about this application?\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Statement |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | It receives an exception, and it is incomplete. |\\r\\n| B | It receives no exception, and it is complete. |\\r\\n| C | It receives a standard award, and it is complete. |\\r\\n| D | Its review is not complete, and it receives an exception. |\\r\\n| E | It receives an exception, and it is complete. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: E.** Let G = grant, A = approval, R = completed review, K = complete application, S = standard award, E = exception. Diagram G → A → R. The no rule is K̸ → R̸, so R → K. The guarantee is R → S or E. The grant establishes R and K. Since S is false, E must hold. Both parts of choice E follow.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the others fail: A contradicts the application\'s completeness. B denies the required remaining award alternative. C contradicts the stated absence of a standard award. D contradicts the completed review established by the grant.\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage: all four map groups across universal wording, requirements, unless and no; guarantees; contraposition; OR elimination. Application completeness and review completeness are deliberately distinct conditions.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Small changes to the drill screen\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Keep the existing visual identity and paper-first step. Put the premises in a compact numbered stack. In missing-link mode, separate the target conclusion with a short divider. In Must Be True mode, show only the question until choices are revealed.\\r\\n2. Enter through the four-world selector, then use the selected world name as the small in-drill label: General Conditionals, Linking, Missing Link, or Must Be True. Show progress within a short set, such as 3 of 10. Keep the front page largely intact.\\r\\n3. Show exactly five authored choices with exactly one correct option for these new modes. Shuffle their order once per item within each saved run, and retain that order on resume and review. Do not generate choices by mechanically reversing one stored conditional. In missing-link mode, a choice equivalent to the correct bridge is also sufficient and cannot be a distractor.\\r\\n4. After commitment, show one primary explanation: original premises, any needed contrapositives, then the finished chain. Highlight the inserted rule in missing-link mode. For and/or, show stacked conditions or a branch with the written connector. Do not introduce grouping brackets.\\r\\n5. Put “Why my choice fails” beside the explanation, using the chosen distractor\'s specific error. Keep extra explanation expandable so the screen does not show several competing answer panels.\\r\\n6. Keep original attempt, retry and revealed answer distinct in saved history. Save each student run, including repeated and unfinished runs. Resume the exact item and reveal state. Teacher preview stays separate from student records.\\r\\n7. Add wording-coverage and operation tags internally. A student can understand letters while still misreading only, unless or no. Do not collapse those into one mastery score.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Logical validation performed\\r\\n\\r\\nAll 20 final formalizations were checked by exhaustive Boolean valuation. For each Must Be True item, exactly one of its five options holds in every model of the premises. For each missing-link item, the existing premises do not already establish the conclusion, exactly one option makes it follow, and the augmented premises are consistent. The intended starting condition is satisfiable for the selected bridges, so the answer is not based on making the conclusion vacuously true.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe check caught equivalent and indirectly sufficient draft distractors; those were replaced before this draft was finalized. Natural-language scope was reviewed against the formalization. This is logical content validation, not browser testing or evidence that the application has been updated.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe next content expansion should vary the existing structures across every indicator family and include dedicated no/not-both and AND/OR contraposition items. These 20 questions establish the two requested modes; they are not a complete mastery curriculum.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n## All exercises from Joshua\'s six supplied photos\\r\\n\\r\\nAdded 2026-09-16. These 12 photographed prompts are required content for **Missing Link**. The earlier ten original missing-link items and ten Must Be True items remain intact. Total draft inventory is now 32 entries: 22 Missing Link and 10 Must Be True. This count includes deliberate equivalent structures; it is not a count of 32 independent skills.\\r\\n\\r\\nSource: user-supplied photos of the section *Missing Links in Conditional Chains as Sufficient Assumptions*. The book\'s title and author are not established by the images. Photos 1–3 establish the first four prompts and their solutions; photos 3–6 establish the remaining eight. Repeated worked solutions are not counted as additional exercises. Several faint negation strokes were checked against the solution pages.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe premise/conclusion pairs below transcribe the photographs. Five-choice adaptations and explanations are newly authored. The photos use an open blank for the sufficient assumption; the app retains Joshua\'s requested multiple-choice interaction. All 12 must be available; the earlier proposal of ten questions per sitting is not a limit on this required content, and session length remains an open design choice.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Read the target, orient the premises, then connect the gap\\r\\n\\r\\nShow each premise on its own line, followed by the target conclusion and “Which additional conditional makes the conclusion follow?” The student diagrams on paper before revealing five choices. A correct option may present the bridge in its contrapositive form. Never show a bridge and its equivalent contrapositive as two separate choices in a single-answer question.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter commitment, show the needed bridge, its equivalent contrapositive, and the completed chain. Those are two forms of one answer, not competing answers. Do not expose either before submission.\\r\\n\\r\\n### PHOTO-ML01: One premise\\r\\n\\r\\nPremise 1: A → B\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: A → C\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → B |\\r\\n| B | B → C |\\r\\n| C | B → C̸ |\\r\\n| D | B̸ → C |\\r\\n| E | A → B |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: B.** A reaches B. Add B → C to reach the target C. The completed chain is A → B → C.\\r\\n\\r\\nEquivalent answer forms: **B → C** and **C̸ → B̸**. Only one form appears among the five choices.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the other choices fail:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Choice A: A is true; B is true; C is false. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice C: A is true; B is true; C is false. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice D: A is true; B is true; C is false. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice E: A is true; B is true; C is false. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n### PHOTO-ML02: One premise\\r\\n\\r\\nPremise 1: A → B\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: C → B\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | A → C |\\r\\n| B | C → A̸ |\\r\\n| C | C̸ → A |\\r\\n| D | C → B̸ |\\r\\n| E | A̸ → C̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: E.** The target starts at C, but the existing rule starts at A. Add C → A. The completed chain is C → A → B.\\r\\n\\r\\nEquivalent answer forms: **C → A** and **A̸ → C̸**. The correct choice uses the contrapositive form. Only one form appears among the five choices.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the other choices fail:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Choice A: A is false; B is false; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice B: A is false; B is false; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice C: A is false; B is false; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice D: A is false; B is false; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n### PHOTO-ML03: One premise\\r\\n\\r\\nPremise 1: A → B\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: C → A̸\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → B̸ |\\r\\n| B | B̸ → C |\\r\\n| C | C → B |\\r\\n| D | C̸ → B̸ |\\r\\n| E | B → A |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: A.** The target ends at A̸. Contrapose the premise to get B̸ → A̸. Add C → B̸, producing C → B̸ → A̸.\\r\\n\\r\\nEquivalent answer forms: **C → B̸** and **B → C̸**. Only one form appears among the five choices.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the other choices fail:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Choice B: A is true; B is true; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice C: A is true; B is true; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice D: A is true; B is true; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n- Choice E: A is true; B is true; C is true. The premises and this option are true, but the target conclusion is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n### PHOTO-ML04: One premise\\r\\n\\r\\nPremise 1: A → B\\r\\n\\r\\nConclusion: B̸ → C\\r\\n\\r\\n| Choice | Additional conditional |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| A | C → A̸ |\\r\\n| B | A̸ → C̸ |\\r\\n| C | C̸ → A |\\r\\n| D | A → C |\\r\\n| E | A → B̸ |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer: C.** The target starts at B̸. Contrapose the premise: B̸ → A̸. Add A̸ → C to obtain B̸ → A̸ → C.\\r\\n\\r\\nEquivalent answer forms: **A̸ → C** and **C̸ → A**. The correct choice uses the contrapositive form. Only one form appears among the five choices.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy the other choices fail:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Choice A: A is false; B is false; C is false. Every incorrect option has an explicit counterexample above. Equivalent answer choices were excluded, and correct positions vary.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis extends the saved draft only. It does not change the deployed activity, replace the existing General Conditionals questions, or settle the remaining interview choices about pacing and progression.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 20.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f0cfed","wall_time_seconds":9.7301089,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2144,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-07-18\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\" — \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Best,\\r\\n> Joshua\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Apology first, as the opening line.** \\"I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\" The ownership sits in the first two sentences, before any explanation.\\r\\n2. **State their position plainly, as a confirmation.** \\"To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet.\\" Answer the question they actually asked. Don\'t mention the package or credits unless the situation calls for it.\\r\\n3. **One-sentence explanation, then \\"That is fixed now.\\"** No mechanics, no system names.\\r\\n4. **Hand them the next step, plus the manual fallback.** The link, \\"pick any time it shows and it will book,\\" and the offer to do it by hand: \\"If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\"\\r\\n5. **Invite the next report:** \\"If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\"\\r\\n6. **Close with \\"Once again, my apologies.\\"** then \\"Best,\\" and \\"Joshua\\".\\r\\n\\r\\nCut from the draft, do not reuse:\\r\\n- \\"Sorry for the runaround\\" as a sign-off. The apology opens the email; it is not a closing quip.\\r\\n- \\"You will get a calendar invite with the Google Meet link right after.\\" Do not narrate what the system will send; it reads as a promise about machinery.\\r\\n- \\"You have all 10 sessions\\" / \\"nothing was used from your package.\\" Leave the package out unless it is needed; the reassurance they want is that nothing is booked, not an accounting.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Situational micro-templates\\r\\n\\r\\nVerbatim-grounded reference lines for the situations that recur most. Adapt, never paste blindly.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Student reschedules / cancels:** low-pressure, no guilt.\\r\\n  > No worries at all. It happens, so please don\'t worry about it. If you would like to reschedule, just let me know.\\r\\n- **He missed / no-showed (approved make-good template):** full ownership, real reason, offer to make it right (see the apology move above). When the cause was an unexpected health issue, the approved shape is: sincerely apologize, state that a serious health scare unexpectedly took him out of commission, offer a **completely free, full LSAT session**, and hand them the complimentary booking link `https://cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/complimentary-session`. Emphasis stays limited: bold the complimentary offer, italicize the line that one focused session can put points on the board. Draft in Gmail; never send without Joshua\'s explicit go-ahead.\\r\\n- **Consultation reminder (day-of):**\\r\\n  > Just checking in before our consultation today. Looking forward to speaking with you. I just wanted to make sure everything is still good.\\r\\n- **Consultation follow-up:** warm, specific to their situation.\\r\\n  > Really nice meeting you. I enjoyed our conversation ... I\'m glad we got to talk through where you\'re at and what you\'re aiming for on test day.\\r\\n- **Inbound prospect (hasn\'t booked):** state availability plainly, no overselling, hand them the next step.\\r\\n  > Thanks for reaching out ... I\'d be happy to talk more about what you\'re working on and see if we\'d be a good fit. You can schedule a consultation here.\\r\\n- **Student under real-life stress:** meet the person first.\\r\\n  > Please don\'t apologize ... it sounds like you are dealing with an incredibly stressful and demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f5823e","wall_time_seconds":10.0059036,"session_id":51254,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}]

')] if p else re.findall(r'^Premise \\d+: (.+)',body,re.M)\n if id=='MBT10': premises=body.strip().split('\\n\\n')[0].split('. '); premises=[p.rstrip('.')+'.' for p in premises]\n if id=='ML8': premises.append(\"For this exercise, evaluate the person's sole pass. C = canceled; X = expired; V = valid; E = permitted to enter using that pass.\")\n c=re.search(r'^Conclusion: (.+)',body,re.M)\n answer=re.search(r'\\*\\*Answer: ([A-E])\\.\\*\\* (.*?)(?=\\n\\nCoverage:|\\Z)',body,re.S)\n explanation=answer.group(2).strip().replace('**','')\n q=dict(id=id,mode=mode,level='variables' if id.startswith('PHOTO') or int(re.search(r'\\d+',id).group())<=6 else 'words',premises=premises,prompt=\"What's the missing link?\" if mode=='missing-link' else 'Which statement must be true?',choices=[dict(id=a,text=t.strip()) for a,t in re.findall(r'^\\| ([A-E]) \\| (.+?) \\|$',body,re.M)],correctChoiceId=answer.group(1),explanation=explanation)\n if c:q['conclusion']=c.group(1).rstrip('.')\n cov=re.search(r'Coverage: (.+)',body)\n if cov:q['groupTags']=re.findall(r'\\b[1-4]\\b',cov.group(1).split(';')[0]) if 'all four' not in cov.group(1) else ['1','2','3','4']\n questions.append(q)\nassert len(questions)==32\nout=pathlib.Path('src/features/activity-centre/conditionals');out.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n(out/'questions.json').write_text(json.dumps(questions,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+'\\n',encoding='utf-8')\nprint([(q['id'],len(q['choices'])) for q in questions])\n'@ | python -",workdir:"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-conditional-update",max_output_tokens:1500}));

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-conditional-update/src/features/activity-centre/conditionals/content.ts\n+import authoredQuestions from './questions.json';\n+\n+export type ConditionalMode = 'linking' | 'missing-link' | 'must-be-true';\n+\n+export interface ConditionalQuestion {\n+  id: string;\n+  mode: ConditionalMode;\n+  level: 'variables' | 'words';\n+  premises: string[];\n+  conclusion?: string;\n+  prompt: string;\n+  choices: { id: string; text: string }[];\n+  correctChoiceId: string;\n+  explanation: string;\n+  groupTags?: string[];\n+}\n+\n+function linking(\n+  id: string,\n+  level: ConditionalQuestion['level'],\n+  premises: string[],\n+  conclusion: string,\n+  choices: string[],\n+  correctChoiceId: string,\n+  explanation: string,\n+  groupTags?: string[],\n+): ConditionalQuestion {\n+  return {\n+    id, mode: 'linking', level, premises, conclusion,\n+    prompt: \"What's the missing link?\",\n+    choices: choices.map((text, index) => ({ id: 'ABCDE'[index], text })),\n+    correctChoiceId, explanation, groupTags,\n+  };\n+}\n+\n+const linkingQuestions: ConditionalQuestion[] = [\n+  linking('LINK1', 'variables', ['P → Q', 'R → S'], 'P → S',\n+    ['R → Q', 'Q → R', 'S → R', 'Q → P', 'S → P'], 'B',\n+    'P gives Q. The next rule starts at R. Add Q → R to connect P → Q → R → S. The other choices point back along the chain instead of connecting its open ends.'),\n+  linking('LINK2', 'variables', ['P → Q', 'S̸ → R̸'], 'P → S',\n+    ['Q → R̸', 'R → Q', 'Q̸ → R', 'R̸ → Q̸', 'S → P'], 'D',\n+    'Contrapose the second premise to get R → S. The gap is Q → R. Choice D gives its equivalent contrapositive, R̸ → Q̸. Together the rules give P → Q → R → S.'),\n+  linking('LINK3', 'variables', ['P → Q̸', 'R → S', 'S → T'], 'P → T',\n+    ['Q → R', 'R → Q̸', 'Q̸ → R', 'T → R', 'R̸ → Q̸'], 'C',\n+    'P gives Q̸, so the connection must start with Q̸. Add Q̸ → R. The completed chain is P → Q̸ → R → S → T. Matching a letter without matching its negation does not connect the rules.'),\n+  linking('LINK4', 'variables', ['P → Q', 'Q → R', 'S → T'], 'T̸ → P̸',\n+    ['S → R', 'R̸ → S', 'T → P', 'R → S̸', 'S̸ → R̸'], 'E',\n+    'The target is the contrapositive of P → T. Connect R → S to get P → Q → R → S → T. Choice E states that same bridge as S̸ → R̸. The target then follows by contraposition.'),\n+  linking('LINK5', 'words', ['Whenever a learner registers, a learner account is created.', 'Every verified account receives library access.'],\n+    'Every learner who registers receives library access.',\n+    ['Every created learner account is verified.', 'Every verified account was created for a registered learner.', 'Library access guarantees registration.', 'Every registered learner has an unverified account.', 'Library access requires registration.'], 'A',\n+    'Registration gives an account. Verification gives library access. The missing connection is account created → verified. Add it to get registration → account created → verified → library access. The other choices do not establish verification from account creation.', ['1', '2']),\n+  linking('LINK6', 'words', ['A project can launch only if funding is approved.', 'A completed review guarantees authorization.'],\n+    'Every project that can launch has authorization.',\n+    ['Authorization requires approved funding.', 'A completed review guarantees approved funding.', 'Approved funding guarantees a completed review.', 'Every project without approved funding has a completed review.', 'Authorization guarantees a completed review.'], 'C',\n+    'Launch → approved funding. Completed review → authorization. Add approved funding → completed review. This connects launch → approved funding → completed review → authorization. = Record<string, boolean>;\n+\n+/** Accepts the bank's unambiguous letter notation, never arbitrary natural language. */\n+export function parseExpression(source: string): Expression {\n+  const text
